- **specflow**: Write or continue project documents with the specflow skill ([vuquoctrungbk/specflow](https://github.com/vuquoctrungbk/specflow/blob/HEAD/adapters/opencode/commands/specflow.md))
- **specforge-analyze**: Audit the active feature's artifacts against each other and report gaps. Read-only — it produces findings, it does not change code. Delegate to the `reviewer` subagent for an independent pass. ([pooyagolchian/specforge](https://github.com/pooyagolchian/specforge/blob/HEAD/dist/agents/cursor/.cursor/commands/specforge-analyze.md) · ★1)
- **specforge-approve**: Record a human's approval of a stage. Approvals are deliberate human acts — this skill is **user-invoked only** and never auto-triggers. Approving **both** `specify` and `plan` for the active feature is what unblocks code edits (the PreToolUse gate). ([pooyagolchian/specforge](https://github.com/pooyagolchian/specforge/blob/HEAD/dist/agents/cursor/.cursor/commands/specforge-approve.md) · ★1)
- **specforge-clarify**: Convert the spec's `[NEEDS CLARIFICATION]` markers into concrete decisions using a **questions-in-a-file** flow (from AI-DLC): more reviewable and diffable than chatting, and it keeps a record. Run this between `/specforge-specify` and `/specforge-plan`. ([pooyagolchian/specforge](https://github.com/pooyagolchian/specforge/blob/HEAD/dist/agents/cursor/.cursor/commands/specforge-clarify.md) · ★1)
- **specforge-constitution**: Define the small set of engineering principles this project commits to. `/specforge-plan` checks every plan against them and must document compliance or an explicit exception. These are **soft gates** (advisory) — the hard, blocking gate is spec + plan approval. ([pooyagolchian/specforge](https://github.com/pooyagolchian/specforge/blob/HEAD/dist/agents/cursor/.cursor/commands/specforge-constitution.md) · ★1)
- **specforge-implement**: Execute `tasks.md` top-to-bottom to implement the active feature. This is deliberately **user-invoked only** (not auto-triggered) so building is an explicit decision. The PreToolUse gate will refuse code edits unless the feature's `specify` **and** `plan` are approved — so if you haven't approved yet, do that first. ([pooyagolchian/specforge](https://github.com/pooyagolchian/specforge/blob/HEAD/dist/agents/cursor/.cursor/commands/specforge-implement.md) · ★1)
- **specforge-init**: Create the committed `specforge/` workspace that every other SpecForge command reads and writes. Run this once per project. It is safe to re-run: never overwrite an existing file — only create what is missing and report what already existed. ([pooyagolchian/specforge](https://github.com/pooyagolchian/specforge/blob/HEAD/dist/agents/cursor/.cursor/commands/specforge-init.md) · ★1)
- **specforge-loop**: Drive the active feature's `tasks.md` to completion by executing tasks one at a time in a loop, checking each off as you go. This is SpecForge's take on the **Ralph loop** (run the agent repeatedly until the work is done) — but made **safe**: it can only ever execute a plan a human already approved, and it stops instead of guessing when it hits a blocker. ([pooyagolchian/specforge](https://github.com/pooyagolchian/specforge/blob/HEAD/dist/agents/cursor/.cursor/commands/specforge-loop.md) · ★1)
- **specforge-operate**: Close the lifecycle. After a feature is built, turn it into something that can be **shipped and owned**: a concrete deploy plan, a runbook, observability, a rollback plan, and a handoff. This is AI-DLC's **Operations** phase. It *plans* operations — it does **not** execute deployments. ([pooyagolchian/specforge](https://github.com/pooyagolchian/specforge/blob/HEAD/dist/agents/cursor/.cursor/commands/specforge-operate.md) · ★1)
- **specforge-plan**: Translate an approved spec into a concrete technical plan. This is the **Construction** design step. Consider delegating to the `architect` subagent. The human's approval of this plan is what **unblocks code edits** (together with the spec approval). ([pooyagolchian/specforge](https://github.com/pooyagolchian/specforge/blob/HEAD/dist/agents/cursor/.cursor/commands/specforge-plan.md) · ★1)
- **specforge-quick**: The escape hatch that keeps SpecForge from becoming ceremony for a one-line fix. It flips `fasttrack: on` so the gate allows code edits without a full spec+plan — and records that choice in the audit log. **User-invoked only.** ([pooyagolchian/specforge](https://github.com/pooyagolchian/specforge/blob/HEAD/dist/agents/cursor/.cursor/commands/specforge-quick.md) · ★1)
- **specforge-resume**: Reconstruct context from the on-disk state (which survives context compaction) and continue the lifecycle at the first unfinished step. ([pooyagolchian/specforge](https://github.com/pooyagolchian/specforge/blob/HEAD/dist/agents/cursor/.cursor/commands/specforge-resume.md) · ★1)
- **specforge-reverse-engineer**: Most real adoption is on existing code, not greenfield. This skill analyzes the current repo and produces a baseline set of artifacts so the team can move forward under the lifecycle. Delegate the heavy analysis to the `reverse-engineer` subagent to keep the main context clean. ([pooyagolchian/specforge](https://github.com/pooyagolchian/specforge/blob/HEAD/dist/agents/cursor/.cursor/commands/specforge-reverse-engineer.md) · ★1)
- **specforge-specify**: Turn a feature request into a testable specification. This is the **Inception** phase: describe the problem, users, and requirements — *not* the implementation. ([pooyagolchian/specforge](https://github.com/pooyagolchian/specforge/blob/HEAD/dist/agents/cursor/.cursor/commands/specforge-specify.md) · ★1)

Auto-numbers, links to the current commit, prompts for status, drivers, options, and consequences. The ADR becomes part of the project's durable memory — read by /speckit-context-refresh and enforced by /speckit-decision-audit. ([Satcomx00-x00/speckit-python](https://github.com/Satcomx00-x00/speckit-python/blob/HEAD/.claude/commands/speckit-decision-new.md))
- **speckit-decision-supersede**: Mark an existing ADR as superseded and scaffold its replacement. Preserves the audit trail — the old ADR keeps its content, gains a `superseded_by` link, and the index updates its status. The new ADR carries a back-reference and inherits the old ADR's constitution_refs unless overridden. ([Satcomx00-x00/speckit-python](https://github.com/Satcomx00-x00/speckit-python/blob/HEAD/.claude/commands/speckit-decision-supersede.md))

([Satcomx00-x00/speckit-python](https://github.com/Satcomx00-x00/speckit-python/blob/HEAD/.claude/commands/speckit-docs-sync.md))

FIRST runs an interactive clarification round (up to 5 high-impact questions, each with a recommended default) to remove ambiguity, then walks a layered slice: typed contracts (Pydantic/dataclass) → domain models → repository Protocol + adapter → pure Result-returning services (dependency-injected) → interface layer (API route / CLI command / library function) → wiring & side effects → pytest unit + integration tests. - **speckit.debt.log**: Record a technical debt as an entry under .specify/debt/&lt;slug&gt;/ — where it lives in the code, what it costs while unpaid, and what paying it looks like. Use when the user wants to log, register, note or track technical debt, a shortcut, a hack, a TODO or FIXME worth remembering, a 'we will fix it later', or anything deliberately left imperfect — and right after an implementation took a shortcut worth writing down. Reads code, never writes it. ([leolicona/guide-me](https://github.com/leolicona/guide-me/blob/HEAD/.specify/extensions/debt/commands/speckit.debt.log.md))
- **speckit.debt.pay**: Close a technical debt entry under .specify/debt/&lt;slug&gt;/ only after verifying against the code that its exit condition is met, recording the evidence in payment.md with a verdict of verified, partial, unpaid or not-run. Use when the user says a debt was paid, fixed, resolved, cleaned up or removed and wants the register updated, asks whether a debt is still there, or after a refactor or cleanup PR lands. Does not write the fix itself — that goes through the normal spec or lite-path flow. ([leolicona/guide-me](https://github.com/leolicona/guide-me/blob/HEAD/.specify/extensions/debt/commands/speckit.debt.pay.md))
- **speckit.debt.review**: Re-check every open technical debt entry under .specify/debt/ against the current codebase and write a snapshot to .specify/debt/review.md — which debts are still there, moved, already gone, or have spread.
